<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove that a discrete group is amenable if and only if every uniformly bounded representation on a complex Hilbert space is similar to a unitary representation. This resolves Dixmier's unitarizability problem affirmatively for discrete groups.

- **C1** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): A discrete group is amenable if and only if it is unitarizable. For every discrete nonamenable group $G$ and every $\varepsilon>0$, there are a complex Hilbert space $\mathcal K$ and a representation $\pi:G\to\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal K)$ with $\|\pi\|\le1+\varepsilon$ that is not similar to a unitary representation. If $G$ is countable, $\mathcal K$ may be chosen separable.
  - Explanation: Amenability is characterized by group unitarizability; nonamenable discrete groups admit nonunitarizable witnesses with arbitrarily small excess over the unitary uniform bound.
- **C2** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-kernel-obstruction)): Let $G$ be a countable group, let $k$ be a positive integer, and let $A,T\in\mathcal B(\mathcal H_k)$ with $A\in U_k(G)'$. Write $$A[x,y]=W(x^{-1}y),\qquad E=\sum_{s\in G}\left\lVert W(s)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^2.$$ Suppose that, for some $K\ge0$, $$\left\lVert\operatorname{row}_e(T)\right\rVert\le K, \qquad \left\lVert\operatorname{col}_e(A-T)\right\rVert\le K.$$ Then $E$ is finite and, for every $C\in U_k(G)'$, $$\label{eq:kernel-energy-obstruction} E\le 4k\bigl(K+\left\lVert T-C\right\rVert\bigr)^2.$$ Consequently, $$\label{eq:kernel-distance-obstruction} \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}\bigl(T,U_k(G)'\bigr) \ge \frac12\sqrt{\frac{E}{k}}-K,$$ where $\mathop{\mathrm{dist}}(T,U_k(G)')=\inf_{C\in U_k(G)'}\left\lVert T-C\right\rVert$.
  - Explanation: Invariant kernel energy controls distance from the translation commutant through a finite-dimensional row and column estimate.
- **C3** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-direct-sum-obstruction)): Let $G$ be a countable group. For each $j\in\mathbb N$, let $k_j$ be a positive integer, put $$\mathcal H_j=\ell^2(G;\mathbb C^{k_j}), \qquad (U_j(g)\xi)(x)=\xi(g^{-1}x),$$ and let $A_j,T_j\in\mathcal B(\mathcal H_j)$ with $A_j\in U_j(G)'$. Write $$A_j[x,y]=W_j(x^{-1}y),\qquad E_j=\sum_{s\in G}\left\lVert W_j(s)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^2.$$ Suppose that one constant $K\ge0$ satisfies $$\begin{aligned} \left\lVert\operatorname{row}_e(T_j)\right\rVert&\le K, & \left\lVert\operatorname{col}_e(A_j-T_j)\right\rVert&\le K &&(j\in\mathbb N),\\ \left\lVert T_j-U_j(g)T_jU_j(g)^{-1}\right\rVert&\le K &&&&(j\in\mathbb N,\ g\in G). \end{aligned}$$ Assume also that $$\frac{E_j}{k_j}\longrightarrow\infty.$$ Then $G$ has a representation on a separable complex Hilbert space whose operators are bounded and invertible, with $$\sup_{g\in G}\left\lVert\pi(g)\right\rVert\le1+K,$$ and which is not similar to a unitary representation. No uniform bound on $\left\lVert T_j\right\rVert$ or $\left\lVert A_j\right\rVert$ is assumed.
  - Explanation: Diverging energy per fibre dimension obstructs a bounded implementer on a direct sum and yields a nonunitarizable triangular representation.
- **C4** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-contracting-average)): If $G$ is a nonamenable discrete group, there are a finite set $S\subset G$ containing $e$, with $d=\|S\|\ge2$, and a number $0<\rho<1$ such that $$\left\lVert\frac 1d\sum_{t\in S}R_t\right\rVert\le\rho.$$
  - Explanation: Nonamenability supplies a strictly contracting finite average of right translations.
- **C5** (construction, [source](#stmt-prop-sparse-splitting)): Let $G$ be countable and nonamenable, and fix $S,d,\rho$ as in [lem:contracting-average](#lem:contracting-average). For every $\ell\ge1$, list the products of $\ell$ elements of $S$, with multiplicity, as $s_1,\ldots,s_n$, where $n=d^\ell$, and put $r=\lceil n\rho^\ell\rceil$. There is a function $a:G\times\{1,\ldots,n\}\to\{0,1\}$ such that $$\label{eq:sparse-mask} \#\{i:a(x,i)=1\}\le r\quad(x\in G),\qquad \#\{i:a(ys_i^{-1},i)=0\}\le r\quad(y\in G).$$
  - Explanation: Labelled convolution edges admit an endpoint assignment with bounded row and column loads, retaining product multiplicities.
- **C6** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-frames)): Let $n\ge 2$ and $r\ge 1$ be integers, and put $$\label{eq:frame-parameters} p=\min(2r,n),\qquad k=\left\lceil 2r\log(2n)\right\rceil, \qquad b_0=10,$$ where $\log$ denotes the natural logarithm. There are unit vectors $v_1,\ldots,v_n\in\mathbb C^k$ such that, for every $J\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\}$ with $\|J\|\le p$, the operator $$V_J\colon\ell^2(J)\longrightarrow\mathbb C^k, \qquad V_J z=\sum_{i\in J}z_i v_i,$$ satisfies $\left\lVert V_J\right\rVert\le b_0$.
  - Explanation: Unit vectors can be chosen so every small subfamily has a uniformly bounded synthesis operator.
- **C7** (bound, [source](#stmt-lem-masked)): In the preceding setting, let $1\le p\le n$ and $b_0>0$. Suppose that $$\left\lVert V_J\right\rVert\le b_0 \quad\text{for every }J\subseteq\{1,\ldots,n\} \text{ with }\|J\|\le p,$$ where $V_J$ is the synthesis operator with columns $v_i$, $i\in J$. For a mask $c$ as in [eq:masked-operator](#eq:masked-operator), define $$\mathcal R_x=\{i:c(x,i)\ne0\},\qquad \mathcal C_y=\{i:c(ys_i^{-1},i)\ne0\}.$$ If $\|\mathcal R_x\|\le p$ for every $x\in G$ and $\|\mathcal C_y\|\le p$ for every $y\in G$, then $$\left\lVert T_c\right\rVert\le b_0^2.$$
  - Explanation: Sparse masks on both endpoints give a norm bound through analysis, multiplication and synthesis on indexed edges.
- **C8** (bound, [source](#stmt-cor-block-rows-columns)): Under [eq:weights-orientation-bounds](#eq:weights-orientation-bounds), one has $$\left\lVert q_xT\right\rVert\le b_0^2\quad(x\in G), \qquad \left\lVert(A-T)\iota_y\right\rVert\le b_0^2\quad(y\in G).$$
  - Explanation: Endpoint loads control the individual block rows and complementary block columns.
- **C9** (bound, [source](#stmt-prop-uniform-differences)): Under [eq:weights-orientation-bounds](#eq:weights-orientation-bounds), the maps $U(g)$ in [eq:weights-A-T-U](#eq:weights-A-T-U) form a unitary representation of $G$, and $A$ commutes with every $U(g)$. Define $$D(g)=T-U(g)TU(g)^{-1}\quad(g\in G).$$ Then $$\label{eq:uniform-difference-bound} \sup_{g\in G}\left\lVert D(g)\right\rVert\le b_0^2=100.$$ Moreover, $D(e)=0$ and $$\label{eq:weights-cocycle} D(gh)=D(g)+U(g)D(h)U(g)^{-1}\quad(g,h\in G).$$
  - Explanation: Differences between an assignment and its left translates form a uniformly bounded operator cocycle.
- **C10** (construction, [source](#stmt-lem-subgroup-induction)): Let $H$ be a subgroup of a discrete group $G$, and let $\sigma:H\to\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal K)$ be a uniformly bounded representation on a complex Hilbert space. There is a uniformly bounded representation $\Pi:G\to\mathrm{GL}(\mathcal L)$ on $\mathcal L=\ell^2(G/H;\mathcal K)$ such that $$\|\Pi\|=\|\sigma\|.$$ If $\Pi$ is unitarizable, then $\sigma$ is unitarizable.
  - Explanation: Induction preserves the uniform representation bound and passes nonunitarizability from a subgroup to the ambient group.
- **C11** (conditional_result, [source](#pm-h-11)): Let $G$ be any discrete group and let $\pi:G\to\mathrm{GL}(H)$ be a uniformly bounded nonunitarizable representation on a complex Hilbert space. The operator-norm convergent sum $$T_\pi(f)=\sum_{g\in G}f(g)\pi(g),\qquad f\in\ell^1(G),$$ defines a bounded complex-linear unital homomorphism for convolution, with $\left\lVert T_\pi(f)\right\rVert\le\|\pi\|\left\lVert f\right\rVert_1$; absolute summability justifies multiplication of the sums. Nevertheless, its restriction to $\mathbb C[G]$ has no bounded complex-linear unital algebra-homomorphism extension to either the full group algebra $C^*(G)$ or the reduced group algebra $C_r^*(G)$. Indeed, a full extension $\Phi$ would be similar to a $*$-homomorphism by the companion similarity theorem (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) [[OpenAIKadison2026](#ref-OpenAIKadison2026)]: some bounded invertible $S$ would make $S\Phi(\cdot)S^{-1}$ a unital $*$-homomorphism. For each canonical group unitary $u_g$, one has $\Phi(u_g)=\pi(g)$, so every $S\pi(g)S^{-1}$ would be unitary, a contradiction. A reduced extension would compose with the canonical unital $*$-quotient $C^*(G)\to C_r^*(G)$ to give a full extension. Thus the automatic $\ell^1$-norm bound does not imply $C^*$-norm continuity. Applying this observation to the witnesses in [thm:main](#thm:main) gives such a failure for every nonamenable discrete group, even with uniform representation bounds arbitrarily close to one.
  - Explanation: The integrated representation is bounded for the group convolution algebra, but no bounded extension to either group operator algebra exists under the companion similarity theorem.

Write $$A_j[x,y]=W_j(x^{-1}y),\\qquad\n  E_j=\\sum_{s\\in G}\\left\\lVert W_j(s)\\right\\rVert_{\\mathrm{HS}}^2.$$ Suppose that one constant $K\\ge0$ satisfies $$\\begin{aligned}\n  \\left\\lVert\\operatorname{row}_e(T_j)\\right\\rVert&\\le K, &\n  \\left\\lVert\\operatorname{col}_e(A_j-T_j)\\right\\rVert&\\le K\n  &&(j\\in\\mathbb N),\\\\\n  \\left\\lVert T_j-U_j(g)T_jU_j(g)^{-1}\\right\\rVert&\\le K\n  &&&&(j\\in\\mathbb N,\\ g\\in G).\n\\end{aligned}$$ Assume also that $$\\frac{E_j}{k_j}\\longrightarrow\\infty.$$ Then $G$ has a representation on a separable complex Hilbert space whose operators are bounded and invertible, with $$\\sup_{g\\in G}\\left\\lVert\\pi(g)\\right\\rVert\\le1+K,$$ and which is not similar to a unitary representation. No uniform bound on $\\left\\lVert T_j\\right\\rVert$ or $\\left\\lVert A_j\\right\\rVert$ is assumed.",
          "source_id": "PM-251.1"
        }

        "exact_quote": "Let $H$ be a subgroup of a discrete group $G$, and let $\\sigma:H\\to\\mathrm{GL}(\\mathcal K)$ be a uniformly bounded representation on a complex Hilbert space.       "evidence": [
        {
          "anchor": "pm-h-11",
          "exact_quote": "Nevertheless, its restriction to $\\mathbb C[G]$ has no bounded complex-linear unital algebra-homomorphism extension to either the full group algebra $C^*(G)$ or the reduced group algebra $C_r^*(G)$.",
          "source_id": "PM-251.1"
        }
      ],
      "exact_statement": "Let $G$ be any discrete group and let $\\pi:G\\to\\mathrm{GL}(H)$ be a uniformly bounded nonunitarizable representation on a complex Hilbert space. The operator-norm convergent sum $$T_\\pi(f)=\\sum_{g\\in G}f(g)\\pi(g),\\qquad f\\in\\ell^1(G),$$ defines a bounded complex-linear unital homomorphism for convolution, with $\\left\\lVert T_\\pi(f)\\right\\rVert\\le|\\pi|\\left\\lVert f\\right\\rVert_1$; absolute summability justifies multiplication of the sums. Nevertheless, its restriction to $\\mathbb C[G]$ has no bounded complex-linear unital algebra-homomorphism extension to either the full group algebra $C^*(G)$ or the reduced group algebra $C_r^*(G)$. Indeed, a full extension $\\Phi$ would be similar to a $*$-homomorphism by the companion similarity theorem (OpenAI 2026, Theorem 1.1) [[OpenAIKadison2026](#ref-OpenAIKadison2026)]: some bounded invertible $S$ would make $S\\Phi(\\cdot)S^{-1}$ a unital $*$-homomorphism. For each canonical group unitary $u_g$, one has $\\Phi(u_g)=\\pi(g)$, so every $S\\pi(g)S^{-1}$ would be unitary, a contradiction. A reduced extension would compose with the canonical unital $*$-quotient $C^*(G)\\to C_r^*(G)$ to give a full extension. Thus the automatic $\\ell^1$-norm bound does not imply $C^*$-norm continuity. Applying this observation to the witnesses in [thm:main](#thm:main) gives such a failure for every nonamenable discrete group, even with uniform representation bounds arbitrarily close to one.",
      "explanation": "The integrated representation is bounded for the group convolution algebra, but no bounded extension to either group operator algebra exists under the companion similarity theorem.",

<a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **Uniformly bounded representation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A representation $\pi:G\to\mathrm{GL}(H)$ of a discrete group on a complex Hilbert space is *uniformly bounded* if $$\|\pi\|:=\sup_{g\in G}\left\lVert\pi(g)\right\rVert<\infty.$$
- **Unitarizable representation** ([source](#sec:introduction)): The representation is *unitarizable* if some bounded invertible $S$ makes every $S\pi(g)S^{-1}$ unitary.
- **Unitarizable group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A group is unitarizable when every uniformly bounded representation on every complex Hilbert space is unitarizable.
- **Amenable discrete group** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A discrete group $G$ is *amenable* if there is a positive linear functional $m:\ell^\infty(G)\to\mathbb C$ with $m(1)=1$ and $m(L_gf)=m(f)$, where $(L_gf)(x)=f(g^{-1}x)$.
- **Bounded operator cocycle** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A bounded operator cocycle is a map $D:G\to\mathcal B(\mathcal H)$ with $\sup_g\left\lVert D(g)\right\rVert<\infty$ and $$D(gh)=D(g)+U(g)D(h)U(g)^{-1}.$$
- **Inner cocycle** ([source](#pm-h-4)): It is *inner* if one bounded operator $B$ satisfies $D(g)=B-U(g)BU(g)^{-1}$ for all $g$.
- **Translation Hilbert space and representation** ([source](#sec:obstruction)): For a countable group $G$ and a positive integer $k$, put $$\mathcal H_k=\ell^2(G;\mathbb C^k), \qquad (U_k(g)\xi)(x)=\xi(g^{-1}x).$$
- **Operator blocks, row and column** ([source](#sec:obstruction)): For $F\in\mathcal B(\mathcal H_k)$, write $$F[x,y]=\iota_x^*F\iota_y,\qquad \operatorname{row}_e(F)=\iota_e^*F,\qquad \operatorname{col}_e(F)=F\iota_e.$$
- **Rank-one weights** ([source](#pm-h-8)): Let $v_1,\ldots,v_n\in\mathbb C^k$ be unit vectors and put $$P_i=v_i v_i^*,\qquad \mathcal H=\ell^2(G;\mathbb C^k).$$
- **Masked block operator** ([source](#pm-h-8)): For a function $c\colon G\times\{1,\ldots,n\}\to\mathbb C$ with $\|c(x,i)\|\le1$, define $$\label{eq:masked-operator} (T_c\xi)(x)=\sum_{i=1}^n c(x,i)P_i\xi(xs_i), \qquad \xi\in\mathcal H.$$

| id | quantity | relation | value | regime |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Q1 | \|\pi\| | ≤ | $1+\varepsilon$ | Witness representations on complex Hilbert spaces. |
| Q2 | E | ≤ | $4k\bigl(K+\left\lVert T-C\right\rVert\bigr)^2$ | Every bounded operator in the translation commutant. |
| Q3 | \mathop{\mathrm{dist}}\bigl(T,U_k(G)'\bigr) | ≥ | $\frac12\sqrt{\frac{E}{k}}-K$ | Distance to the bounded translation commutant. |
| Q4 | \sup_{g\in G}\left\lVert\pi(g)\right\rVert | ≤ | $1+K$ | All group elements in the resulting direct-sum representation. |
| Q5 | d | ≥ | $2$ | Finite averaging sets containing the identity. |
| Q6 | \left\lVert\frac 1d\sum_{t\in S}R_t\right\rVert | ≤ | $\rho$ | A finite averaging set and a strictly positive contraction factor below unity. |
| Q7 | n | = | $d^\ell$ | Positive product length; multiplicities retained. |
| Q8 | r | = | $\lceil n\rho^\ell\rceil$ | Positive product length and assigned endpoint loads. |
| Q9 | p | = | $\min(2r,n)$ | Maximum subfamily size. |
| Q10 | k | = | $\left\lceil 2r\log(2n)\right\rceil$ | Fibre dimension; natural logarithm. |
| Q11 | b_0 | = | $10$ | Synthesis norms for all permitted subfamilies. |
| Q12 | \left\lVert T_c\right\rVert | ≤ | $b_0^2$ | All masks satisfying the displayed loads. |
| Q13 | \sup_{g\in G}\left\lVert D(g)\right\rVert | ≤ | $100$ | Every left translation of the assignment. |
| Q14 | \sum_{s\in G}\left\lVert W_\ell(s)\right\rVert_{\mathrm{HS}}^2 | ≥ | $n_\ell$ | Every stage of the convolution product construction. |
| Q15 | \left\lVert T_\pi(f)\right\rVert | ≤ | $\|\pi\|\left\lVert f\right\rVert_1$ | Absolutely summable group coefficients. |

            "source_id": "PM-251.1"
          }
        ]
      },
      "relation": "≤",
      "value_latex": "1+K"
    },

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **R1** [claim](#stmt-lem-kernel-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-kernel-obstruction): Finite-dimensional Hilbert–Schmidt row and column estimates -> Lower bound on distance from the translation commutant
- **R2** [claim](#stmt-prop-direct-sum-obstruction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-direct-sum-obstruction): Bounded cocycle direct sum and invariant-complement contradiction -> Nonunitarizable triangular representation
- **R3** [claim](#stmt-lem-contracting-average) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-contracting-average): Contradiction through weak-star compactness of almost invariant means -> Contracting finite average
- **R4** [claim](#stmt-prop-sparse-splitting) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-sparse-splitting): Convolution edge counts, finite slot matching and an infinite branch -> Sparse assignment to endpoints
- **R5** [claim](#stmt-lem-frames) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-frames): Random signs, finite nets and a union bound -> Unit vectors with bounded small synthesis subfamilies
- **R6** [claim](#stmt-lem-masked) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-masked): Analysis and synthesis through distinct edge coordinates -> Uniform masked operator bound
- **R7** [claim](#stmt-cor-block-rows-columns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-cor-block-rows-columns): Restrict the endpoint mask to an individual row or column -> Block row and complementary block column bounds
- **R8** [claim](#stmt-prop-uniform-differences) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-differences): Subtract a left translated assignment and count changed labels at both endpoints -> Uniformly bounded conjugation cocycle
- **R9** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-uniform-differences-2): Positive kernel energy, vanishing dimension ratio and rescaling before taking a direct sum -> Countable nonamenable witness with prescribed uniform bound
- **R10** [claim](#stmt-lem-subgroup-induction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subgroup-induction): Coset induction and compression of an invariant equivalent inner product -> Induced witness retaining the uniform bound
- **R11** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-subgroup-induction-3): Finitely generated nonamenable subgroup, induction and invariant-mean averaging -> Equivalence for arbitrary discrete groups
- **R12** [claim](#pm-h-11) / [proof](#pm-h-11): Integrate the representation on absolutely summable coefficients and contradict a bounded extension using the companion similarity theorem -> Failure of bounded extension to the full and reduced group operator algebras

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **projection-energy**: Positive rank-one projection weights preserve their diagonal energy contributions, and coincident labels contribute nonnegative cross terms.
- **global-obstruction**: The nonunitarizability obstruction requires a diverging energy-to-dimension ratio and uniform bounds throughout the direct sum.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **finite-surrogate**: Use a finite projection-energy calculation to certify the nonunitarizability witness. -> Finite energy samples do not establish divergence or rule out a bounded global implementer.
- **discard-labels**: Deduplicate coincident group products before forming projection weights. -> Distinct indexed projections are lost, changing the energy and the edge factorization.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `constructive`. The finitely supported projection kernel has a finite exact energy calculation. This computes that calculation for rational real unit vectors while retaining repeated labels; it does not decide amenability or certify the direct-sum obstruction.

```python
# INPUTS: JSON array of cases, each with labels and rational real unit vectors.
# PRECONDITIONS: One to three vectors, dimension one or two; labels zero or one; exact unit norms.
# OUTPUTS: JSON array of exact squared Hilbert-Schmidt energies, as rational strings.
# TIME: O(c*n*k*k) rational operations for c cases, n labels and fibre dimension k.
# SPACE: O(c + k*k) rational entries excluding input storage.
# ARITHMETIC: Certified exact python-flint fmpq arithmetic; no floating point.
# FAILURE: Invalid inputs raise an exception and exit nonzero; no approximate result.
import json, sys
from flint import fmpq
cases = json.load(sys.stdin)
assert isinstance(cases, list) and 1 <= len(cases) <= 600
answer = []
for case in cases:
    labels = case['labels']
    vs = [[fmpq(x) for x in v] for v in case['vectors']]
    n = len(vs)
    assert 1 <= n <= 3 and len(labels) == n
    k = len(vs[0])
    assert k in (1, 2) and all(len(v) == k for v in vs)
    assert all(type(s) is int and s in (0, 1) for s in labels)
    assert all(sum(x*x for x in v) == 1 for v in vs)
    blocks = {}
    for label, v in zip(labels, vs):
        block = blocks.setdefault(label, [[fmpq(0) for b in range(k)] for a in range(k)])
        for a in range(k):
            for b in range(k):
                block[a][b] += v[a]*v[b]
    energy = sum(x*x for block in blocks.values() for row in block for x in row)
    assert energy >= n
    answer.append(str(energy))
print(json.dumps(answer))
```

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **L1** (hypothesis): The equivalence concerns discrete groups. The argument does not supply the corresponding theorem for nondiscrete locally compact groups with strongly continuous representations. Blocked use: ['Extending the equivalence to nondiscrete locally compact groups.']
- **L2** (hypothesis): Group unitarizability quantifies over every uniformly bounded representation on every complex Hilbert space. Unitarizing a selected representation does not establish group amenability. Blocked use: ['Inferring amenability from a similarity for a selected representation.']
- **L3** (edge_case): The countable witness may be separable; the induced witness for an uncountable nonamenable group is nonseparable. Restricting the definition to separable Hilbert spaces changes the scope used by the proof. Blocked use: ['Applying the arbitrary-group conclusion under a separable-only definition of unitarizability.']
- **L4** (counterexample_scope): The witness is asserted for nonamenable groups and positive prescribed error. The statement does not assert a nonunitarizable witness whose uniform bound equals the unitary bound. Blocked use: ['Replacing the positive error in the witness statement by zero.']
- **L5** (hypothesis): The similarity can depend on the representation. A common bound on similarity condition numbers is not an assumption of unitarizability. Blocked use: ['Adding an unstated uniform condition-number requirement to the equivalence.']
- **L6** (nonconstructive): The simultaneous assignment on the countable edge set is obtained through an infinite branch. The proof supplies no finite stopping rule for constructing the full assignment. Blocked use: ['Treating the infinite assignment as a finite executable procedure for arbitrary groups.']
- **L7** (nonconstructive): The frame proof establishes existence through a probability bound and a favorable realization. It does not provide a deterministic search bound for producing the vectors. Blocked use: ['Claiming a deterministic vector-construction runtime from the existence proof.']
- **L8** (counterexample_scope): The direct-sum obstruction concerns the absence of a bounded implementer on the whole direct sum. Each individual cocycle already has a bounded implementer. Blocked use: ['Claiming that an individual finite stage is already the non-inner cocycle required by the obstruction.']
- **L9** (hypothesis): Indexed convolution edges retain their labels when group products coincide. Collapsing parallel edges changes the coordinates used by the factorization and the load counts. Blocked use: ['Discarding multiplicities before using the masked-operator factorization.']
- **L10** (formal_scope): The argument works in ZFC and uses complex Hilbert spaces. No alternate foundational or real-Hilbert-space version is established here. Blocked use: ['Claiming the same proof under unstated foundational restrictions or for real Hilbert spaces.']
- **L11** (hypothesis): The group algebra extension consequence also requires the companion similarity theorem. It is not a consequence established from the group equivalence alone. Blocked use: ['Using the extension obstruction without the additional similarity theorem.']
- **L12** (approximation): A bound in the absolutely summable coefficient norm does not imply continuity in the group algebra norm required for the stated extensions. Blocked use: ['Inferring a bounded extension to either group algebra from the integrated representation bound.']

>
> ## Scope
>
> Dixmier's unitarizability problem asks whether a discrete group is amenable exactly when every uniformly bounded Hilbert-space representation is similar to a unitary one. The formalization establishes this equivalence for every discrete group.
>
> For every nonamenable group and every $\varepsilon>0$, it also gives a nonunitarizable representation with uniform operator bound at most $1+\varepsilon$. The Hilbert space can be chosen separable for countable groups; a separate linked statement records a separable witness with uniform bound $101$ in that case.

Comparator source `DixmierAllDiscrete.lean` (1891 bytes, sha256 `b87f008ef0a48e93cd3946df5729929fff873aa11d5522b1eef05653cf1e773e`), verbatim:

```lean
import Mathlib.Analysis.InnerProductSpace.LinearMap
import Mathlib.Topology.ContinuousMap.Bounded.Normed
import Mathlib.Topology.Instances.Discrete

namespace OAI

noncomputable section
universe u v
namespace Dixmier
open scoped BoundedContinuousFunction

def leftTranslate {G : Type u} [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G]
    [DiscreteTopology G] (g : G) (f : G →ᵇ ℂ) : G →ᵇ ℂ :=
  f.compContinuous ⟨fun x => g⁻¹ * x, continuous_of_discreteTopology⟩

def Amenable (G : Type u) [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G]
    [DiscreteTopology G] : Prop :=
  ∃ m : (G →ᵇ ℂ) →ₗ[ℂ] ℂ,
    m (BoundedContinuousFunction.const G 1) = 1 ∧
    (∀ f : G →ᵇ ℂ, (∀ x, 0 ≤ (f x).re ∧ (f x).im = 0) →
      0 ≤ (m f).re ∧ (m f).im = 0) ∧
    ∀ (g : G) (f : G →ᵇ ℂ), m (leftTranslate g f) = m f

def SimilarToUnitary {G : Type u} [Group G]
    {H : Type v} [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H]
    (π : G →* (H →L[ℂ] H)) : Prop :=
  ∃ S : H ≃L[ℂ] H, ∀ (g : G) (x : H), ‖S (π g (S.symm x))‖ = ‖x‖

def Unitarizable (G : Type u) [Group G] : Prop :=
  ∀ (H : Type v) [NormedAddCommGroup H] [InnerProductSpace ℂ H]
    [CompleteSpace H] (π : G →* (H →L[ℂ] H)),
    (∃ C : ℝ, ∀ g, ‖π g‖ ≤ C) → SimilarToUnitary π

def CurrentMainTheorem : Prop :=
  ∀ (G : Type u) [Group G] [TopologicalSpace G] [DiscreteTopology G],
    (Amenable G ↔ Unitarizable.{u, max u v} G) ∧
    ∀ (ε : ℝ), 0 < ε → ¬ Amenable G →
      ∃ (H : Type (max u v)) (_ : NormedAddCommGroup H) (_ : InnerProductSpace ℂ H)
        (_ : CompleteSpace H) (π : G →* (H →L[ℂ] H)),
        (Countable G → TopologicalSpace.SeparableSpace H) ∧
        (∀ g, ‖π g‖ ≤ 1 + ε) ∧ ¬ SimilarToUnitary π

theorem current_main_theorem : CurrentMainTheorem.{u,v} := by
  sorry

end Dixmier
end

end OAI
```

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove that a discrete group is amenable if and only if every uniformly bounded representation on a complex Hilbert space is similar to a unitary representation. This resolves Dixmier's unitarizability problem affirmatively for discrete groups.

<a id="pm-h-1"></a>
